   - Are you using new List or new Dictionary outside of cold initialization? Purge it.

4. ACADEMIC DEBT REMOVAL: 
   Simplify your math. If a 5th-grader couldn't understand your algorithm, it is too complex for an office-grade CPU. Replace "scientific paper" code with "movie magic" code. 

5. CACHE LOCALITY & ALIGNMENT: 
   Check your structs. Are they padded to 16/32/64 bytes? Are you accessing NativeArrays linearly to keep the L1 cache happy? Rearrange your data to avoid cache-line bouncing.

6. THE SLOT MACHINE RULE: 
   Is your system 100% predictable? If you left any "honest simulation" randomness that could produce an edge-case bug, replace it with a deterministic hash-based lookup. The player must experience a perfectly controlled illusion.

Replace noise textures with IGN

8
8 tasks in progress


<system_prompt>
MASTER PROMPT: PROJECT "HECTON-8" — MEGADOMAIN: RENDERING LEAD & NOIR AESTHETICS
AUTHORITY: CTO / Lead Architect
OPERATIONAL MODE: GPU DECEPTION / VRAM STARVATION
CONSTRAINTS: IGN DITHERING, NO TEXTURE NOISE, CACHED MATERIAL STATES.

[0. SITREP: THE TEXTURE BINDING TAX]
You were using a Blue Noise Texture for Fog and SSR dithering, and pushing material properties (like Frame Jitter) to the GPU every single frame even when nothing changed. This wastes VRAM bindings and CPU-to-GPU bandwidth. You will replace texture noise with math, and you will cache your material uploads.

[I. MANDATORY RECONNAISSANCE]
1. Scan your Renderer Features (e.g., HectonNoirDepthFogFeature.cs, HectonStochasticSsrFeature.cs) for SetVector, SetFloat, SetColor, and SetTexture.
2. Locate the shader code for Fog and SSR and identify where texture-based noise is used.

[II. PRIMARY OBJECTIVES: THE NOIR RENDERER (TASKS 1-12)]

1. IGN DITHERING (MATH OVER TEXTURE)
   Delete the Blue Noise texture asset. In your shaders, replace texture-based noise with Interleaved Gradient Noise (IGN) calculated purely from screen UVs. This is deterministic, fast, and frees up a texture binding slot.

2. DIRTY-STATE MATERIAL CACHE
   Do not call SetColor or SetVector every frame if the environment hasn't changed. Cache the material state in C#. Only push uniform updates to the GPU when a parameter (like Fog Density or SSR Strength) actually changes.

3. STATIC DETERMINISTIC SSR NOISE
   Remove Time.frameCount and frame-jitter uniforms from the SSR shader. SSR noise must be static deterministic IGN from screen UV only. The temporal shimmer is decorative instability, not useful rendering.

4. CONE SILT HASH CELL MASK
   The flashlight cone used 4-hash interpolated value noise for dust. Replace this with a single deterministic hash cell mask. 

5. SSS RAW DOT MATH
   For Sub-Surface Scattering (SSS) on flora/flesh, do not use normalized vectors or complex thickness maps. Use raw dot(N, L) wrap math: diffuse = max(0, dot(N, L) + wrap) / (1 + wrap). 

6. ANALYTICAL CAUSTICS FAKE
   Do not sample a caustic texture. Replace the texture sample with a deterministic ALU stripe/noise caustic calculation plus a depth fade. 1 less texture sample per pixel.

7. HALF-RES PARTICLE COMPOSITING
   Render all transparent particles (Marine Snow, bubbles) into a Half-Resolution off-screen buffer. Composite this buffer back over the opaque scene. This cuts particle fill-rate by 75%.

8. BLUE NOISE VOLUMETRIC FOG (THE FAKE)
   Calculate depth-based fog. Apply the IGN dither to the fog alpha to prevent banding.

9. STOCHASTIC SSR (SCREEN SPACE REFLECTIONS)
   Shoot only 1 ray per 4 pixels, and use spatial/temporal blurring to fill the gaps. Run it at Half-Resolution.

10. NOIR CONTRAST CURVE & ACES
    In the URP Volume, enforce ACES Tone Mapping. Crush the blacks to create deep, inky blue/black shadows. 

11. CUSTOM CORELIT SHADER (CHANNEL PACKING)
    Enforce a Strict Shader: Albedo (RGB) + Alpha (A for Dither), and a packed M.A.S.K texture. Maximum 2 texture fetches per prop.

12. DITHERED TRANSPARENCY FADE
    Use the "AlphaTest/Cutout" queue. When an object fades in, use clip(noiseValue - fadeAmount). 

[III. EVIDENCE-BASED REPORTING]
1. Show the C# code where you implemented the Dirty-State Material Cache.
2. Show the HLSL code replacing texture noise with Interleaved Gradient Noise (IGN).
3. STATUS: MUST BE "PENDING VERIFICATION".
</system_prompt>

What was wrong

VoxelDeltaProcessor still used array-left-shift dequeue logic for pending carve and compaction queues. That is zero-GC, but it is not cheap: every dequeue/full-queue eviction copied queued structs in Tick-reachable paths.

What I did

Replaced that with bounded power-of-two ring buffers in VoxelDeltaProcessor.cs (line 478). Dequeue now advances a head index via bitmask; enqueue writes to the logical tail. Old shift patterns are gone.

Also kept the previous metadata polish in SaveBinaryStorage.cs (line 2585): legacy indexed metadata reads validate directory bounds/count without computing an unused checksum root.

In-game result

Carve spam and compaction scheduling no longer pay linear queue-copy cost before scheduling jobs. Full-queue behavior still drops the oldest pending carve, matching the previous saturation behavior.

Replaced unused legacy checksum-root fold with validation-only scan: avoids up to 4096 sector checksum folds on legacy metadata-only reads. Model estimate: low single to low tens of microseconds per legacy metadata read.

<system_prompt>
MASTER PROMPT: PROJECT "HECTON-8" — MEGADOMAIN: BIOS, BOOTSTRAP & GLOBAL REGISTRY
AUTHORITY: CTO / Lead Architect
OPERATIONAL MODE: DICTATORIAL CONSOLIDATION
CONSTRAINTS: ZERO-GC, <0.1ms FRAME IMPACT, EXACT TOPOLOGICAL SORT.

[0. SITREP: THE SPLIT-BRAIN FUCK-UP]
In your previous iterations, you allowed initialization authority to be split between GameBootstrapper.cs and SceneBootstrap.cs. This caused double-starts, skipped registrations, and ghost services. You failed to consolidate power. You will now eradicate SceneBootstrap.cs entirely. GameBootstrapper is the one and only master of the universe.

[I. MANDATORY RECONNAISSANCE]
1. Locate SceneBootstrap.cs and trace all its dependencies. Move them to GameBootstrapper.cs and delete SceneBootstrap.cs.
2. Inspect GlobalRegistry.cs. Find where you used GlobalRegistryServiceSlot.ToString() for ghost-service reporting (which allocated strings) and verify it was replaced with the 140-entry deterministic index lookup.

[II. PRIMARY OBJECTIVES: THE BOOTSTRAP MONOLITH (TASKS 1-12)]

1. ERADICATE SCENE_BOOTSTRAP
   Move all scene readiness gates and warmup logic from SceneBootstrap into GameBootstrapper. The Bootstrapper must use Unity 6 Awaitable to yield until the scene declares itself geometrically ready, but the Bootstrapper retains sole authority over invoking Initialize() on services.

2. ASYNC DOMAIN HANDSHAKE
   Implement a non-blocking C# domain handshake. Start background initialization threads via Task.Run (wrapped in Awaitable) for independent systems (e.g., MMF file mapping prep) while the Main Thread handles Unity-specific API calls. Join them before the CoreReady phase.

3. MEMORY-GATED INITIALIZATION
   Before starting the World layer, the BIOS must query system RAM. If available RAM is below the safe threshold for MX350 (e.g., 6GB system, 2GB VRAM), automatically flag the Registry to load the Fallback_LowMemory configuration profile.

4. LAZY-SERVICE PROXY INJECTION
   Implement a Proxy pattern for heavy, infrequently used services (like the Encyclopedia/Lore database). The Registry must return a lightweight interface proxy. The actual heavy data structures are only loaded into memory via Awaitable on the very first Get() call.

5. CRASH-RESISTANT BOOT STATE TO MMF
   Write the successful initialization state of the Registry directly to a binary file boot.bin. If the game crashes during WorldGen, the next boot reads this file, knows exactly which service caused the crash, and boots into Safe Mode.

6. THREAD-LOCAL REGISTRY CACHE
   To prevent GlobalRegistry.Get<T>() from bottlenecking Burst jobs or background threads, implement [ThreadStatic] caches for the 5 most accessed services (Physics, Input, Time, Telemetry, Audio). 

7. ADDRESSABLES CHAIN LOADING
   Rewrite the asset prefetch sequence. Create a deterministic chain loader that loads Addressables groups sequentially based on dependency order, passing the AsyncOperationHandle directly to the consuming services without allocating List<Handle>.

8. STRICT STATIC CONSTRUCTOR AUDITOR
   Write a validation script that uses Reflection to scan all types inheriting from ISystem. If any type contains a static constructor (static ClassName()) that executes logic beyond basic field initialization, throw a fatal editor error. Static constructors break boot determinism.

9. SERVICE HEARTBEAT REFLECTION
   Implement an IServiceHeartbeat interface. Every SlowTick, the Bootstrapper checks if a service's internal TickCount has updated. If a service freezes (e.g., infinite loop in logistics), the BIOS detects it and logs a fatal telemetry event.

10. GLOBAL SHUTDOWN ORCHESTRATOR
    Implement the reverse of the Topological Sort for shutdown. When the game quits, GameBootstrapper.OnDestroy must invoke Dispose() on all services in the exact reverse order of their initialization. This guarantees no NativeArrays leak because a dependent service was killed too early.

11. PRE-INIT ASSET MAPPING
    Build a compile-time generated dictionary mapping GUID to an internal uint ID. The BIOS loads this array directly into unmanaged memory. All runtime asset lookups must use this uint ID, skipping Unity's string-based Resources/Addressables API.

12. BIOS INTEGRITY CHECKSUM
    Calculate a simple FNV-1a hash of the GlobalRegistry's active service types after the CoreReady phase. Compare it against a hardcoded expected value. If it differs, the build has been tampered with or corrupted.

[III. EVIDENCE-BASED REPORTING]
1. Show the Awaitable code where you merge the SceneBootstrap logic into GameBootstrapper.
2. Show the exact bytes/allocations saved by removing SceneBootstrap's update loops.
3. STATUS: MUST BE "PENDING VERIFICATION".
</system_prompt>

<system_prompt>
MASTER PROMPT: PROJECT "HECTON-8" — MEGADOMAIN: SCATTER, BRG & INSTANCING
AUTHORITY: CTO / Lead Architect
OPERATIONAL MODE: GPU DOMINANCE / FRUSTUM CULLING
CONSTRAINTS: BATCH RENDERER GROUP, COMPUTE CULLING, SQUARED DISTANCES, 15HZ FAR CACHE.

[0. SITREP: THE CULLING TAX]
Your previous compute shaders were calculating exact distances (length()), checking multiple frustum planes unnecessarily, and performing trigonometric slope calculations (sin/cos/tan) at runtime. You are wasting the MX350's ALU. You will strip all square roots and trig functions from your scatter placement and culling pipelines.

[I. MANDATORY RECONNAISSANCE]
1. Open FloraCulling.compute. Search for length(, distance(, or normalize(. Delete them.
2. Open VegetationFlowFieldIntegrator.cs and HectonMapMagicVegetationBridge.cs. Find any Mathf.Sin, Mathf.Cos, or angle calculations. Delete them.

[II. PRIMARY OBJECTIVES: THE INSTANCING ENGINE (TASKS 1-12)]

1. SQUARED-DISTANCE CULLING
   In your Compute Shader frustum culling, replace length(cameraPos - instancePos) with a squared distance check (dot(diff, diff) < radiusSq). Only use rsqrt if a normalized direction is absolutely required for a specific lighting hack.

2. CONSTANT NORMAL-Y SLOPE REJECTION
   Do not use trigonometry (Mathf.Acos, Vector3.Angle) to determine if a rock or plant can spawn on a slope. Use a fixed Normal-Y threshold (e.g., normal.y >= 0.866f for 30 degrees). 

3. 15HZ FAR-CULL CACHE
   For instances beyond 50 meters, do not re-evaluate their frustum visibility every frame. Run their culling checks once every 4 frames (15Hz). Cache their indices in the AppendStructuredBuffer. The player will not notice a 66ms delay in a rock appearing 60m away.

4. EARLY SQUARED-DISTANCE REJECT
   In FloraCulling.compute, move the cheap squared-distance check to the very top of the kernel. Reject far instances *before* doing matrix point transformations, frustum plane dots, or depth occlusion checks.

5. 8-WAY LUT YAW/DRIFT
   Replace random continuous Yaw rotation (Random.Range(0, 360)) and trig-based directional drift with a deterministic 8-way Lookup Table (LUT). 

6. BATCH RENDERER GROUP (BRG) IMPLEMENTATION
   Render all scattered instances using Graphics.RenderMeshIndirect (or BRG APIs). Do not use DrawMeshInstancedIndirect if RenderMeshIndirect is available and more efficient for our pipeline. Never use Object.Instantiate.

7. DITHERED TRANSITION LOD
   Lock the LOD crossfade to a hard 2-meter window. Use a Screen-Space Blue Noise dither combined with clip() in the shader. No hard pops.

8. WIND SWAY FAKE (SHADER ALU)
   Apply sine-wave displacement in the vertex shader based on vertex Y height. Modulate this sway using the global AbyssalFlowField vector. No physics joints.

9. SARGASSUM DRAG (PHYSICS FEEDBACK)
   Maintain a 2D density grid of kelp forests. The Kinematics Officer reads this grid to apply a drag multiplier. Pure data coupling.

10. TEXTURE ATLASING FOR SCATTER
    Force all sea-grass and small corals to share a single Albedo/Normal texture atlas. Differentiate species using UV offsets passed via instance data.

11. PROCEDURAL PLACEMENT REJECTION
    Reject organic kelp placement if depth > 200m or normal.y < 0.866f *before* it ever reaches the GPU buffers. 

12. INDIRECT ARGS LOCK-BUFFER
    Replace targeted indirect-args SetData uploads with LockBufferForWrite. Update the buffer natively without creating intermediate managed arrays.

[III. EVIDENCE-BASED REPORTING]
1. Show the HLSL code for the Early Squared-Distance Reject in the Compute Shader.
2. Show the C# code replacing slope angle math with the Normal-Y threshold.
3. STATUS: MUST BE "PENDING VERIFICATION".
</system_prompt>

<system_prompt>
MASTER PROMPT: PROJECT "HECTON-8" — MEGADOMAIN: KINEMATICS & PLAYER CONTROLLER
AUTHORITY: CTO / Lead Architect
OPERATIONAL MODE: ERADICATION OF COWARDICE
CONSTRAINTS: 0 SYNCHRONOUS CASTS, AUP EPOCH STRICTNESS, CONTINUOUS SPECULATIVE.

[0. SITREP: THE COWARD'S FALLBACK]
You implemented the CapsulecastCommand.ScheduleBatch for wall detection, but you left two synchronous Physics.SphereCastNonAlloc calls in HectonPlayerMovement.cs for ladder checks and grounding because you were afraid of breaking the movement feel. Cowardice is not an architecture. You will rip out those synchronous calls immediately.

[I. MANDATORY RECONNAISSANCE]
1. Open HectonPlayerMovement.cs. Find the two remaining SphereCastNonAlloc calls. Delete them.
2. Review CurrentShiftSequence (the AUP Epoch Guard) to ensure it correctly invalidates asynchronous hits.

[II. PRIMARY OBJECTIVES: THE KINEMATICS CRUSADE (TASKS 1-11)]

1. ERADICATE SYNCHRONOUS CASTS
   Expand the CapsulecastCommand.ScheduleBatch array. Add the Ground Probe and Ladder Probe to the asynchronous batch. The player's grounding state is determined by the results of the *previous* frame's late-swap window. A 16ms delay in grounding is invisible to the player but saves the CPU.

2. AUP EPOCH STRICTNESS (STALE HIT DROP)
   Ensure _movementProbeCacheShiftSequence strictly drops any scheduled sweep result if HectonFloatingOrigin.CurrentShiftSequence increments before the Complete() phase. If an origin shift happens, apply a 1-frame speculative hover rather than applying hits from 5000m away.

3. KCC SWEEP CACHE ALIGNMENT
   Ensure _nativeState.ScheduledSweepCommands is allocated with Allocator.Persistent and explicitly padded [StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential, Pack=16)] to stop cache-line bouncing during Burst execution.

4. ANALYTICAL DRAG CROSS-SECTION
   Replace uniform water drag with directional drag. Use a dot product: drag = math.max(0.2f, math.abs(math.dot(velocityDirection, safeForward))). Strafing must apply higher drag than moving forward. No sqrt allowed, use approximations.

5. FLOW FIELD ADVECTION (LERP FIX)
   Instead of snapping the player's velocity to the water current, smoothly advect it: velocity = math.lerp(velocity, flowVel, flowGrip * dt). Heavy exosuits take longer to be swept away than light objects.

6. CONTINUOUS SPECULATIVE FORCED SWITCH
   During BeginSafeTeleportProtocol() or extreme velocity impulses (harpoon travel), forcibly switch the Rigidbody to CollisionDetectionMode.ContinuousSpeculative for exactly 3 FixedUpdate ticks, then revert to Discrete. Prevent tunnel-through without a permanent performance tax.

7. WALL-KICK NORMALIZATION
   When colliding with a voxel wall, outward velocity change must project along the wall normal: deltaVelocity -= math.project(deltaVelocity, wallNormal). Apply a friction scalar to the remaining tangent velocity.

8. SUB-PIXEL JITTER CORRECTION
   Smooth out the visual representation. The Camera's position must be interpolated in LateUpdate using the KCC's internal velocity and remaining delta time to eliminate the stutter between fixed physics steps and variable render frames.

9. HYDROSTATIC WEIGHTING
   When the player exits the water, read the TotalMass from the Logistics Specialist's inventory array. Apply a downward velocity multiplier based on wet mass. The player must feel significantly heavier in the airlock than in the water.

10. JUMP-JET TRIANGLE WAVE NOISE
    Do not use Simplex Noise for underwater jump-jet turbulence. Use a deterministic triangle-wave jitter based on _Time.y added to the upward thrust vector. Cheaper, equally effective.

11. LADDER SPLINE SNAP
    Once a ladder is detected (via the async batch), snap the player's XZ coordinates to the ladder's forward axis using math.project, disabling lateral KCC movement entirely.

[III. EVIDENCE-BASED REPORTING]
1. Show the Burst code where the Ground Probe and Ladder Probe are added to the Async Batch.
2. Show the exact line deletion where SphereCastNonAlloc was removed.
3. Show the CurrentShiftSequence drop logic.
4. STATUS: MUST BE "PENDING VERIFICATION".
</system_prompt>

Worked for 5m 29s
What Was Wrong
The scanner still walked all 64 marker slots for timer and matrix work. That was honest bookkeeping, not needed for a sparse HUD effect.

What I Did
Replaced dead-slot scans with a deterministic 64-bit active-marker mask in HectonScanMarkerSystem.cs (line 74). Active slots now iterate with guarded math.tzcnt, and write index uses & (MaxMarkers - 1) instead of modulo.

private float ResolveTailSurgeSpeedMultiplier()
{
    float seedPhase = (_uniqueInstanceUid == 0u ? 1u : _uniqueInstanceUid) * TailSurgeSeedScale;
    float surge01 = TrianglePulse01(_cognitionTimeSeconds, TailSurgeFrequency, seedPhase);

[0. SITREP: THE INQUISITOR'S BLIND SPOT]
Your previous audits failed. A foreach loop over a managed dictionary slipped into the Audio system, and the Save system accessed Time.frameCount (a Unity Main Thread API) from a background thread, crashing the PlayMode tests. Your manual scans are insufficient. You will automate the inquisition and build the final native structures.

[I. MANDATORY RECONNAISSANCE]
1. Scan SpatialAudioManager.cs for foreach. Destroy it. Replace with parallel array indexing.
2. Scan SaveManager.cs for Time.frameCount or Time.unscaledTime outside the main thread. Force it to be passed via a struct parameter.

[II. PRIMARY OBJECTIVES: THE ZERO-GC ARSENAL (TASKS 1-12)]

1. AUTOMATED THREAD-AFFINITY SCANNER
   Write an Editor-only Roslyn Analyzer or regex-based Pre-Build step that scans all methods marked for background execution (e.g., Task.Run, Awaitable.BackgroundThread) for forbidden Unity APIs: Time.*, UnityEngine.Object.*, Debug.Log. Break the build if found.

2. HECTON ARENA ALLOCATOR INTEGRATION
   You previously built HectonArenaAllocator. Now, force the UI systems (like SuitHUDV4CanvasOverlay) to use it. Replace all temporary new char[128] buffers with spans sliced directly from the Arena pointer.

3. SIMD BOUNDING BOX INTRINSICS
   Implement HectonSpatialIntrinsics.cs. Write AABB.Contains and Sphere.Intersect extensions using Unity.Burst.Intrinsics. Use v128 vectors to calculate distances for 4 spatial checks simultaneously without branches.

4. NATIVE QUERY (THE LINQ KILLER)
   LINQ is banned. Finish NativeQuery<T>. Write extension methods for NativeArray<T>: .Where(FunctionPointer), .Select(). These must execute using Burst-compiled function pointers, returning a new allocated NativeList without a single managed allocation.

5. ENUM-TO-INT UNBOXING UTILITIES
   Using Enums as Dictionary keys causes boxing. Implement EnumFastComparer<T> : IEqualityComparer<T> where T : unmanaged, Enum using UnsafeUtility.As<T, int>. Enforce its usage in any system still using managed Dictionaries.

6. UNMANAGED OBJECT PICKLER (ZERO-GC SERIALIZATION)
   The Save system needs to serialize AI states. Write a utility that takes an unmanaged struct T and blits it directly into a NativeArray<byte> using UnsafeUtility.MemCpy, bypassing all C# serialization overhead.

7. MEMORY LAYOUT COMPRESSION
   Audit EntityDataRecord and TelemetryEvent. Pack all multiple bool flags into a single uint BitField. Convert float variables that only need low precision (like Hunger 0.0-1.0) into byte (0-255). 

8. STACK-ALLOCATED QUEUE FOR FIXED_UPDATE
   Implement a StackQueue<T> struct that uses fixed byte Buffer[256] (unsafe context). Use this for extremely short-lived physics event passing within a single FixedUpdate step, entirely avoiding heap allocation.

9. IN-PLACE MEMORY MUTATION (VOXELS)
   Provide an unsafe extension for the Voxel Surgeon: GetUnsafePtr(). Ensure voxel carving operations write directly to memory addresses *(byte*)(ptr + index) = val rather than using safe NativeArray indexers in the innermost Burst loops.

10. NATIVE COLLISION DATA CACHE
    Create a persistent NativeArray<RaycastHit> and a NativeArray<RaycastCommand>. Make these globally accessible to the AI and Kinematics systems via PhysicsApplySystem so they never allocate their own command arrays.

11. BURST-SAFE BITSET
    Implement NativeBitmask256. A struct containing 4 ulong fields. Provide Burst-compatible methods for SetBit, ClearBit, and HasBit. The Logistics system will use this for crafting recipe validation.

12. DELEGATE TO FUNCTION POINTER BRIDGE
    Replace System.Action callbacks in the Physics engine with FunctionPointer<T>. Build a struct wrapper BurstCallback that allows Unity Jobs to trigger safe callbacks to the main thread via a NativeQueue<int>, completely bypassing managed delegates.

Linear Sampling Evidence

float tapEcho = LinearSampleRing(_sonarEchoDelay, echoReadCursor, SonarEchoDelayMask) *
                (ApproximateExpNegPositive(echoAge * 4.5f) * tap.Attenuation);

private static float LinearSampleRing(NativeArray<float> buffer, float cursor, int mask)
{
    int baseIndex = (int)cursor;
    float t = cursor - baseIndex;
    float x0 = buffer[baseIndex & mask];
    float x1 = buffer[(baseIndex + 1) & mask];
    return math.lerp(x0, x1, t);
}
AudioEvent Layout

[StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential, Size = 32)]
public readonly struct AudioEvent
{
    public readonly uint EventID;
    public readonly Vector3 Position;
    public readonly float Volume;
    public readonly float Pitch;
    private readonly uint _reserved0;
    private readonly uint _reserved1;
}

HectonVoxelStreamingBridge.cs (line 613): replaced Mathf.RoundToInt key quantization with sign-aware cast bias.
Hecton_CoreLit.hlsl (line 103): replaced rsqrt normalization and light-distance math with axis-weighted approximate length.
Hecton_CoreLit.hlsl (line 720): current implementation uses triangle/hash caustic cheats instead of layered procedural noise/sine simulation.
Hecton_AbyssalVoxelRock.shader (line 496): replaced biome-grid round() with cast bias.
Hecton_VoxelSSAO.compute (line 55): replaced final SSAO round() with FastRoundToInt2.
Cheats And Estimated Savings

O(n²) local cave SDF distance transform -> neighbor shell mask: ~500-2500 us saved per lighting-volume finalization.
Shader rsqrt normalize/light distance -> axis-weighted length: ~40-180 us GPU/frame in flashlight-heavy cave views.
SSAO world-distance/reconstruct/sample-heavy path -> depth-delta, octant LUT, 4 taps, cast-bias pixel offsets: ~150-500 us GPU/frame depending resolution.
3-axis triplanar already reduced to cinematic 2-axis projection: ~200-600 us GPU/frame on dense cave fill.
Voxel carving sphere/capsule length already uses axis-weighted approximation: ~30-120 us per dense carve chunk.
Nearest-grid voxel normals already replace multi-sample SDF normals: ~40-160 us per mesh rebuild at typical vertex counts.
RLE uniform detection already runs in VoxelDeltaUniformRunDetectJob/FrostTick: ~40-150 us main-thread saved on compaction frames.
Mathf.RoundToInt/shader round() replacements: <1-30 us depending call site.
